     BF5E BFFC     
4874               cv224
4875               	; 		' HE IS STANDING ON A STEP, so he moves at the STEP's rate: 2 px
4876               	; 		' across and 1 up PER PASS -- the staircase's own slope, and exactly
4877               	; 		' what one phase of esc_tick moves the pattern by.
4878               	; 		'
4879               	; 		' NOT `fdv`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. Everything else in this loop
4880               	; 		' is paced by the frame delta, and pacing him that way too made him
4881               	; 		' outrun the steps the moment a pass cost more than one frame: his
4882               	; 		' feet started planted on a tread and ended floating above it.
4883               	; 		' Pacing the ANIMATION by fdv instead is worse -- four phases cannot
4884               	; 		' be stepped by 2 or 3 without aliasing, and the staircase runs
4885               	; 		' backwards (see esc_tick). The only arrangement that cannot drift
4886               	; 		' is the one where both are driven by the same fixed step, so the
4887               	; 		' rider is clocked by the pass, like the steps he is standing on.
4888               	; 		'
4889               	; 		' THE FIRST FEW PASSES ARE THE STEP ON. Walking on, his feet are on
4890               	; 		' the floor and the bottom tread is 4 to 7 px above them, so he
4891               	; 		' climbs at 2 px a pass until he catches it (the tread is rising at
4892               	; 		' 1, so the gap closes at 1 a pass) and then rides with it. Jumping
4893               	; 		' on he lands ON a tread, so eson is 0 and this never runs.
4894               	; 		IF esy < eson THEN esy = esy + 1
4895 BF60 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESY,r0
     BF62 20F9

5449               	; 	' ---------------------------------------------------- screen crossings
5450               	; 	' EIGHT PIXELS OF HYSTERESIS. Landing on x = 0 after crossing east would
5451               	; 	' put Kelly one step from re-crossing, and the store would re-blit every
5452               	; 	' frame at the seam. Landing on 8 makes the seam four frames of running
5453               	; 	' wide in each direction.
5454               	; 	' THE STORE HAS WALLS. Clamping to 255 put Kelly's 16 px sprite entirely
5455               	; 	' past the right edge of the screen -- he vanished, and since nothing
5456               	; 	' moved him back it read as having walked out of the world and got stuck.
5457               	; 	' 232 leaves him standing against the wall the end templates now draw.

5525               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------ escalator
5526               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
5527               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
5528               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
5529               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
5530               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
5531               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
5532               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
5533               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
5534               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
5535               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
5536               	; 	'
5537               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
5538               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
5539               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
5540               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at
5541               	; 	' the top of one never lands you on the foot of another.

6594               	; 	' is spent as whole pixels. hsp4 = 6 walks 1,2,1,2 (1.5 px/frame); 7
6595               	; 	' walks 1,2,2,2 over four frames (1.75); 8 is a flat 2. Two IFs, not a
6596               	; 	' loop and not a divide: hacc is under 4 on entry and hsp4 is at most 8,
6597               	; 	' so it can never need a third. `%` and `/` both compile to a real
6598               	; 	' TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this runs every frame.
6599               	; 	'
6600               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
6601               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
6602               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
6603               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
6604               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
6605               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
6606               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
6607               	; 	hacc = hacc + hsp4

6896 CE78 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0

6898               	;
6899               	; 	' ======================================================================
6900               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
6901               	; 	' ======================================================================
6902               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
6903               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
6904               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
6905               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
6906               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
6907               	; 	'
6908               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
6909               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
6910               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
6911               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
6912               	; 	' broken.
6913               	; 	'
6914               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
6915               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
6916               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
6917               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
6918               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
6919               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
6920               	; 	'
6921               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
6922               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
6923               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
6924               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
6925               	; coll_obst:
6926               cvb_COLL_OBST
6927               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the
6928 CE7C D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     CE7E 215A     
6929 CE80 0201  20 	li r1,768   ; 3*256
     CE82 0300     
6930 CE84 9001  18 	cb r1,r0
6931 CE86 1302  14 	jeq cv500
6932 CE88 0460  28 	b @cv499
     CE8A CE90     
6933               cv500
6934 CE8C C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0

7474               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
7475               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
7476               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
7477               	; 	'
7478               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
7479               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
7480               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
7481               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
7482               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
7483               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
7484               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.

7608               	; 		' FOUR RUN FRAMES FROM TWO BITS of the animation counter, and no
7609               	; 		' divide -- `/` compiles to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and
7610               	; 		' this is per-frame code. Two adjacent bits give 0,4,8,12.
7611               	; 		'
7612               	; 		' KELLY USES BITS 2 AND 3 AND HARRY USES 3 AND 4, because a stride
7613               	; 		' has to cover about a stride's worth of ground or the figure
7614               	; 		' skates. Kelly runs 4 px a frame, so four frames a pose is 16 px;
7615               	; 		' Harry runs 1.75, so eight frames a pose is 14. Matching the two
7616               	; 		' rates to the two speeds is what keeps both looking like running.
7617               	; 		kq = P_KLEG1

8224 D9B8 0450  20 	b *r0
8225               	;
8226               	; 	' ======================================================================
8227               	; 	' THE SCANNER
8228               	; 	' ======================================================================
8229               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
8230               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
8231               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
8232               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
8233               	; 	'
8234               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
8235               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
8236               	; 	'
8237               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
8238               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
8239               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
8240               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
8241               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
8242               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
8243               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
8244               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
8245               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
8246               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
8247               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
8248               	; esc_tick:
8249               cvb_ESC_TICK
8250               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
8251               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
8252               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
8253               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
8254               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
8255 D9BA D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     D9BC 2149     
8256 D9BE 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     D9C0 0100     
8257 D9C2 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
8258 D9C4 1402  14 	jhe cv623
8259 D9C6 0460  28 	b @cv622
     D9C8 D9DE     
8260               cv623
8261               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
8262 D9CA D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     D9CC 2149     
8263 D9CE 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     D9D0 0700     
8264 D9D2 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
8265 D9D4 1A02  14 	jl cv625
8266 D9D6 0460  28 	b @cv624
     D9D8 D9DE     
8267               cv625
8268 D9DA C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
8269 D9DC 0450  20 	b *r0
8270               cv624
8271               	; 	END IF
8272               cv622
8273               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
8274               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
8275               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
8276               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
8277               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
8278               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
8279               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
8280               	; 	'
8281               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
8282               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
8283               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
8284               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
8285               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
8286               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
8287               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
8288               	; 	escp = escp + 1
8289 D9DE D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     D9E0 20AF     
8290 D9E2 0220  22 	ai r0,256	; 1
     D9E4 0100

8569 DC38 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
8570 DC3A 0450  20 	b *r0
8571               	;
8572               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
8573               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
8574               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
8575               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
8576               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
8577               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
8578               	; 	'
8579               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
8580               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
8581               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
8582               	; 	'
8583               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
8584               	; 	'   +1  BLACK   Kelly -- and the escalator's FOOT, which the manual
8585               	; 	'               draws black anyway, so the diagonal spans both rows
8586               	; 	'   +2  WHITE   Harry
8587               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line
8588               	; 	'
8589               	; 	' Four is the floor: the Kop, the crook, the furniture and the floor
8590               	; 	' lines are four different colours and none of them can share. Shrinking
8591               	; 	' from six bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the instrument and
8592               	; 	' four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor above it or
8593               	; 	' the bottom of the screen.
8594               	; 	'
8595               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
8596               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
8597               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
8598               	; scan_furn:
8599               cvb_SCAN_FURN

9103               	;
9104               	; 	' -- erase both old dots
9105               	; 	' Erase with the mask each dot was DRAWN with, not whatever sdm happens
9106               	; 	' to hold now. Using the current mask clears a bit the dot never set and
9107               	; 	' leaves the real one lit, so the scanner slowly fills with a trail of
9108               	; 	' stale dots -- which reads as the radar being broken rather than as an

9808               	; 	' falls through to the flash -- no GOTO, so the whole routine stays on
9809               	; 	' one traceable path
9810               	; 	' The flash runs off fphs, its OWN counter. Keying it on the parity of a
9811               	; 	' timer that decrements by a VARIABLE delta would freeze the moment the
9812               	; 	' delta happened to be even -- so the warning would die exactly when the
9813               	; 	' frame is busiest, which is when the player needs it most.

     E77A AA16     
9981               	;
9982               	; 	' REPEATED ADDITION, not a multiply, and deliberately so. Two separate
9983               	; 	' hazards meet on this one line: `tsec * bmul.` is invalid because the `.`
9984               	; 	' suffix marks a 16-bit CONSTANT and both of these are variables, and a
9985               	; 	' plain `tsec * bmul` would be an 8-bit product -- a 50-second capture in
9986               	; 	' the x300 band pays 15,000, which is six times past a byte. Multiplying
9987               	; 	' properly would then land on the MPY/r0 hazard, where a 16-bit var read
9988               	; 	' straight after being multiplied returns the product's HIGH word.
9989               	; 	'
9990               	; 	' At most 50 adds, once per capture. Nothing about this is a hot path.
9991               	; 	'
9992               	; 	' The guard matters: a COMPUTED `FOR 1 TO 0` still runs its body once in
9993               	; 	' CVBasic, so catching Harry on the very last tick would pay a full
9994               	; 	' multiplier instead of nothing.
9995               	; calc_bonus:
9996               cvb_CALC_BONUS
9997               	; 	#bonus = 0

     E77E 20D4

10231               	;
10232               	; 	' ======================================================================
10233               	; 	' SOUND
10234               	; 	' ======================================================================
10235               	; 	' Every effect gets an explicit note-off. SOUND latches -- with no
10236               	; 	' `SOUND ch,f,0` the last tone sustains for ever. And the second argument
10237               	; 	' is a 10-BIT DIVISOR, max 1023: SMALLER IS HIGHER, so a rising sweep is
10238               	; 	' written as a falling number and anything over 1023 is silently masked
10239               	; 	' to an unrelated pitch.
10240               	; sfx_tick:
10241               cvb_SFX_TICK

10585               	; 	RETURN
10586 EC96 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
10587 EC98 0450  20 	b *r0
10588               	;
10589               	; 	' The font stays in bank 0: it is small, and keeping one readable thing
10590               	; 	' unbanked makes a bank-selection mistake obvious (text survives, art does
10591               	; 	' not) instead of producing a uniformly blank screen.
10592               	; 	INCLUDE "font.bas"
10593               	; 	' Keystone Kapers font -- GENERATED by assets/genfont.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
10594               	; 	'
10595               	; 	' Characters 32-90 (59 of them), 8 bytes each = 472 bytes. Williams' cabinet face:
10596               	; 	' heavy, squared, flat terminals, no serifs -- drawn 5x7 inside the 8x8 cell so a
10597               	; 	' blank column at the right and a blank row at the bottom keep it legible.
10598               	; 	'
10599               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits. The run must be CONTIGUOUS, so slots
10600               	; 	' with no glyph emit blanks rather than being skipped -- a gap would shift every
10601               	; 	' letter after it.
10602               	;
10603               	; font_bits:
10604               cvb_FONT_BITS
10605               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00	' space
10606 EC9A 0000     	byte >00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00
     EC9C 0000     
     EC9E 0000     
     ECA0 0000     
10607               	; 	DATA BYTE $20,$20,$20,$20,$20,$00,$20,$00	' !
10608 ECA2 2020     	byte >20,>20,>20,>20,>20,>00,>20,>00
     ECA4 2020     
     ECA6 2000     
     ECA8 2000

     ECAC 0000     
     ECAE 0000     
     ECB0 0000

     ECB4 5050     
     ECB6 F850     
     ECB8 0000

10736               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
10737               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
10738               	; 	'
10739               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
10740               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
10741               	; 	' being small sprites.
10742               	; 	'
10743               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
10744               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
10745               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
10746               	; 	'
10747               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
10748               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
10749               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
10750               	;
10751               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
10752               cvb_SPR_KELLY

11447               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
11448               	; 	' Chars 160-207: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
11449               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
11450               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
11451               	;
11452               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
11453               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
11454               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
11455               	; 	' unreadable.
11456               	;
11457               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then furniture grey / Kelly black / Harry white / floor line yellow per 4 px level, on dark green
11458               cvb_SCAN_COL3

11521               	; 	'   150 EXITC

11525               	; 	'   154 EDHALF
11526               	; 	'   155 ENDWALL
11527               	; 	'   156 SCANBK
11528               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
11529               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
11530               	; 	'
11531               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
11532               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
11533               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
11534               	; 	'
11535               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
11536               	; 	' blit is
11537               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
11538               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
11539               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
11540               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
11541               	; 	'
11542               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
11543               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
11544               	; 	'
11545               	; 	' template ids:
11546               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
11547               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
11548               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
11549               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
11550               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
11551               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF
11552               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF_W
11553               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF_E
11554               	; 	'   8 T_END_W
11555               	; 	'   9 T_END_E
11556               	;
11557               	; stor_tpl:	' 10 templates x 160 bytes
11558               cvb_STOR_TPL
